THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

thu hẹp cuối năm, phản ánh chi phí 
nguyên liệu cao hơn trong 1Q2026

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu/LNST 2Q2026 đạt lần lượt 1,320/367 tỷ VND (+1%/+11% YoY). Lũy kế 
6M2026, doanh thu đạt 2,777 tỷ VND (+3% YoY) trong khi LNST ước đạt 671 tỷ VND 
(+9% YoY). Biên lãi gộp 6M2026 cải thiện đạt 47.0% (+240 bps YoY), chủ yếu do việc 
tăng giá bán giúp doanh nghiệp bù đắp được mức tăng của chi phí đầu vào.

Lũy kế 6M2026, sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck. Việc 
BMP thu hẹp các chương trình chiết khấu, với mức chiết khấu bình quân khoảng 
5.2% trong 2Q2026, làm giảm động lực nhập hàng của các đại lý và khiến sản lượng 
tiêu thụ chậm lại. Dù vậy, thị phần toàn quốc của BMP ước đạt 28% (+1 đpt svck) 
nhờ các doanh nghiệp khác trong ngành gặp khó khăn khi giá hạt nhựa tăng mạnh.

Biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP dự kiến thu hẹp trong 2H2026 trước áp lực chi phí 
nguyên liệu đầu vào tăng. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi kỳ vọng mức độ ảnh hưởng sẽ thấp 
hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành do (1) các lô nguyên liệu giá cao từ 
1Q2026 đã được đưa vào sản xuất và tiêu thụ một phần trong 1H2026; (2) BMP chủ 
động nâng gấp đôi hàng tồn kho để ứng phó với biến động bất lợi của giá nguyên 
liệu; (3) hơn 90% nhu cầu nguyên liệu uPVC được cung ứng từ các đối tác trong 
nước như hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam, AGC Chemicals và LSP; và (4) công ty duy 
trì giá bán bình quân ở mức cao.

Dựa trên kết quả kinh doanh và định giá, chúng tôi khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP với cổ 
phiếu BMP, giá mục tiêu là 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu (tương ứng với mức tăng 14.3% 
so với giá đóng cửa ngày 23/09/2026).

Tăng trưởng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất Hoạt động kinh doanh

Nhựa Bình Minh (BMP) là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực sản xuất ống nhựa với thị phần 
dẫn đầu tại thị trường nội địa (27% vào cuối 
2023), thị trường chính tập trung tại khu vực 
miền Nam. BMP có 4 nhà máy với tổng công suất 
đạt 150,000 tấn/năm. 
Nguồn: BMP, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Kỳ vọng đà phục hồi của xây dựng dân dụng và bất động sản miền Nam sẽ thúc đẩy sản lượng tiêu 
thụ tăng trưởng trong 2026–2027. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Giá PVC được kỳ vọng sẽ hạ nhiệt và khả năng giữ giá bán đầu ra ở mức cao giúp BMP giữ biên lợi 
nhuận gộp ở nền cao.

### Page 4

4

Nhu cầu nhập hàng thận trọng và 
cắt giảm các đợt chiết khấu trong 
kỳ của BMP làm giảm sản lượng tiêu 
thụ trong 2Q2026

Sự hồi phục của nhu cầu xây dựng 
dân dụng và nguồn cung các dự án 
bất động sản tại miền Nam sẽ thúc 
đẩy tăng trưởng sản lượng 2H2026 
 Sản lượng tiêu thụ trong 2Q2026 ước đạt 20,100 tấn, giảm 11% svck, nâng tổng 
sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck do các yếu tố bất 
lợi từ bên ngoài như (1) giá hạt nhựa tăng cao khiến thị trường dè chừng trong 
việc nhập hàng vì lo ngại nguồn cung bất ổn (2) BMP đã thu hẹp các chương 
trình chiết khấu so với 2025 ngay trước thời điểm xung đột bùng nổ, giúp tỷ lệ 
chi phí bán hàng/doanh thu về mức 11.3%, giảm 800 bps so với 1Q2026 ở mức 
19.3%, nhưng đồng thời cũng khiến sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP suy giảm do 
chính sách bán hàng thắt chặt hơn với các đối thủ có thị phần thấp hơn.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP dần hồi phục từ 3Q2026 khi nhu 
cầu nhập hàng của các đại lý cải thiện sau giai đoạn thận trọng trong nửa đầu 
năm. Về cuối năm, hoạt động xây dựng dự kiến tăng tốc khi điều kiện thi công 
thuận lợi hơn sau mùa mưa. Bên cạnh đó, việc triển khai các dự án nhà ở tại 
TP.HCM và các tỉnh lân cận được kỳ vọng hỗ trợ nhu cầu ống nhựa trong giai 
đoạn tới. Theo đó, chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP lần lượt đạt 
96,695/100,125/96,750 tấn trong 2026/2027/2028.

Biểu đồ 3. Tương quan giữa tỷ lệ chi phí bán hàng/doanh 
thu và lợi nhuận thuần/sản lượng tiêu thụ

### Page 5

5

Lợi thế từ hệ sinh thái công ty mẹ 
SCG và đối tác Nhật Bản giúp BMP 
có nhiều dư địa để nâng cao hiệu 
quả hoạt động

Theo chia sẻ từ ban lãnh đạo, BMP có lợi thế vì nhập nguyên liệu từ công ty mẹ 
là hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam và đối tác Nhật Bản là AGC Chemicals Vietnam, 
giúp giảm được 5% thuế nhập khẩu và chi phí vận chuyển. Điều này giúp BMP 
hưởng lợi kép khi có thể tăng được giá bán theo thị trường nhưng nguyên vật 
liệu rẻ hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành. KBSV nhận thấy định hướng 
dài hạn của BMP là cân bằng lợi ích cổ đông và khách hàng, vì vậy việc giảm giá 
bán để giành thị phần không phải lựa chọn ưu tiên của doanh nghiệp mà là 
nâng cao tỷ suất lợi nhuận/sản phẩm thành phẩm.

KBSV dự báo biên lợi nhuận gộp thu hẹp trong 2H2026 khi chi phí nguyên liệu 
giá cao từ 1H2026 được phản ánh vào giá vốn, và hồi phục trong 2027–2028 
nhờ đặc tính giữ giá bán ít thay đổi của nhóm nhựa. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá PVC 
resin chịu áp lực giảm từ (1) nhu cầu xây dựng tại Trung Quốc còn yếu khi thị 
trường bất động sản chưa hồi phục rõ ràng; và (2) các nhà sản xuất giải phóng 
hàng tồn kho sau mùa mưa, làm tăng sản lượng hạt nhựa PVC resin. Theo đó, 
KBSV dự phóng biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP lên 46.4%/46.7%/46.5% trong 
2026/2027/2028.

Document 296: First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-10-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-10-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-25
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change
of OCR call
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:19 October 22 2026 Share
Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter and 3.7% y/y. Core inflation
pressures are elevated and market pricing is primed for a 25bp OCR hike next week, which
we now expect.
Consumers Price Index, September quarter 2026 – key stats

### Page 2

Headline inflation
Quarterly change: +0.7% (prev: +1.5%)
Westpac forecast: +0.7%
RBNZ: +0.8%
Market median: +0.8%, range +0.5% to +1.2%

Annual change: +3.7% (prev: +4.1%)
Westpac forecast: +3.7%, RBNZ: +3.9%, Market: +3.8%

Non-tradables
Quarterly change: +1.0% (prev: +0.6%)
Westpac forecast: +1.3%
Annual change: +3.3% (prev: +3.4%)

Tradables
Quarterly change: +0.2% (prev: +2.7%)
Westpac forecast: -0.2%
Annual change: +4.3% (prev: +4.9%)

Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter. That saw the annual inflation rate easing to
3.7%, down from 4.1% in the year to June. 
Today’s result was in line with our forecast, but lower than the RBNZ’s forecast for 3.9% annual
inflation. 
However, under the surface, the details were more concerning. Measure of core inflation have taken a
step higher, with several closely watched measures now at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s
target band. 
We suspect that today’s result will leave the market continuing to price a strong chance of another
OCR hike at next Wednesday’s policy review. In that light, we think the RBNZ will regard this as the
path of least resistance, and we now expect that they will deliver (previously we expected that they
would pause and assess, before hiking at the December MPS).

What contributed to inflation in the September quarter?
Underpinning September’s 0.7% rise in consumer prices were some big price movements in both
directions. 
The largest move in prices this quarter was actually a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by
any stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for both petrol and diesel over the September
quarter were lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle East

### Page 3

conflict. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91 unleaded was 6.5% lower in September than in
the June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower. Those moves took 0.4ppts off inflation
this quarter. 
While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully
high. And that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other goods and services. Of note, the
September quarter saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with international airfares up 14%
and domestic airfares up nearly 8%. 
The September quarter also saw the annual increases in local council rates (up 6.2%) and charges for
services like water supply (up 7.5%). That continues the run of large increases seen in recent years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices, largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
prices for fresh vegetables. 
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in housing rents, which are the largest component of
the CPI, accounting for 11.2% of the basket. With low population growth and ample supply, rents were
flat in the September quarter and have effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual terms,
rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Annual and core inflation 
The annual inflation rate dropped back to 3.7% in the September quarter, down from 4.1% in the year
to June and the highest level in two years.
Looking under the surface, prices in the domestically oriented non-tradables group rose 1.0% over the
quarter, less than expected. That saw annual non-tradables inflation nudging down to 3.3% (from 3.4%
in the year to June). 
Non-tradables inflation has continued to run at above-average rates over the past year. That’s despite
the softness in economic growth and the labour market. In large part, that’s due to the continued
sizeable increases in administered prices, such as electricity charges which rose 9.3% over the past
year. 
However, prices in other areas have also been firm. Non-tradables excluding housing and utilities costs
(which also omits local council rates) was 3.4% for the year. In seasonally adjusted terms, quarterly
non-tradables inflation has been tracking around 0.8% for most of the past year. It was noted that in
sectors like construction, higher materials costs are adding to prices. Feedback from business we've
heard indicates that those higher materials costs are likely related to high oil prices. 
On the imported front, lower fuel costs saw tradable prices rising by just 0.2% over the September
quarter (though above our forecast for a small fall). That saw annual tradables inflation dropping back
to 4.3%, down sharply from the 4.9% rise in the year to June. 
While fuel costs have been a key driver for tradables inflation, under the surface, other imported
inflation pressures are looking firm. Excluding food and fuel costs, tradables prices were up 2.7% over
the past year. That’s the highest level in three years. 
With big swings in fuel costs, and concern that this is spilling over into operating costs more generally,
a key focus for the RBNZ is what’s been happening to measures of core inflation (which smooth
through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual prices and instead track the underlying trend in

### Page 4

Related articles
inflation). 
On this front we did some see worrying signs. Several closely watched measures of core inflation have
picked up, and now sit at the top of (or slightly above) the RBNZ target band. In terms of specifics, we
saw: 
Reinforcing this picture of firm underlying inflation pressures, a growing number of goods have been
increasing in price. That could be an early indication that businesses, even those with a limited
exposure to fuel costs, are starting to factor in expectations of continued high inflation when setting
prices.

Outlook 
While today’s result was lower than the RBNZ assumed in their September MPS projections, the
firmness in core inflation will give the RBNZ pause for thought ahead of their policy review on 28
October. Furthermore, with a sharp rise in petrol prices in recent weeks, September’s easing in annual
inflation is likely to be just a temporary reprieve. We expect headline inflation to reaccelerate back over
4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain above the RBNZ’s current forecast for most of
2027. 
Market pricing and views have coalesced around an October hike which we now suspect the MPC will
see as the path of least resistance. Core inflation measures are now close to or above 3%, which will
add fuel to arguments from the MPC hawks. We will have more detail in our MPR Preview later today,
including on the implications for the shape of the tightening cycle moving forward.

CPI ex-fuel inflation: +3.0% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
CPI ex-fuel and food: 3.2% yr (vs +2.9% previously)
CPI ex-fuel, food and household energy: +2.9% yr (vs +2.5% previously)
30% trimmed mean: +3.0% (vs +2.6% previously)
Weighted median: +3.1% (vs +2.7% previously)

Document 299: Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us

Source PDF: all report pdf/Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

From better ﬁscal headroom to surprise income tax hikes, this
is how UK Chancellor John Healey's ﬁrst budget could surprise
investors
James Smith
UK Chancellor John Healey
In a nutshell: What we expect
Higher debt interest costs look set to drive the level of 'ﬁscal
headroom' from £24bn in March to £10bn. There's a growing
sense that Chancellor John Healey would like to get this up
to £15bn or higher. The government is set to modestly
increase spending on the cost of living, though the total

intervention is unlikely to meaningfully top £5bn and it's
likely to be mostly billed as temporary to avoid counting
against the main ﬁscal rule. We expect an increase in capital
spending of roughly £10bn/year, bearing in mind this is
exempt from the ﬁscal rule dictating that the current budget
must be in surplus after ﬁve years. To rebuild headroom

### Page 2

against that rule, we expect £5-6bn of tax hikes, likely via a
higher Bank Surcharge and possibly broadening the National
Insurance tax base.
In short, we don't expect the budget to deliver a material
stimulus (or add materially to the already-planned ﬁscal
consolidation). The impact on the Bank of England, which is
poised to hike rates in November, is likely to be fairly
minimal.
But all chancellors like to keep a rabbit in their hat – and what
follows are ﬁve ways it could surprise us.

1. Fiscal headroom goes up – not down
What if the UK’s ﬁscal hole isn’t nearly as bad as we think?
The widespread expectation going into the UK’s Autumn Budget on
28 October is that the Chancellor is going to have to face up to
higher debt interest costs with some modest tax hikes.
Bank of England rate expectations are almost a percentage point
higher than they were at the time of the Oﬃce for Budget
Responsibility’s March assessment of Britain’s public ﬁnances. 10-

year bond yields are some 70 basis points higher, which, taken
together, will push up debt interest spending by £11bn/year by
2029/30.
That points to a sizeable reduction in the amount of money the
government has left under its main ﬁscal rule, which requires the
current budget (day-to-day spending, including debt interest less
revenue) to be in surplus within three years. Back in March, that
surplus, or ﬁscal headroom, was projected at £24bn in 2029/30.

Debt interest costs are rising

*Based on the average rate in the 10 days up to 25 September, which
likely represents the OBR's observation window for this budget
Source: Macrobond, ING
We think that headroom will have dropped to around £10bn before
any corrective action. And the vibe from the Treasury suggests the
Chancellor would prefer it to be above £15bn, to give more
breathing space in case of future adverse forecast revisions. That
would point to some limited tax hikes.
But what if that’s all wrong? The truth is, nobody really knows how

the OBR will change its assessment of the UK economy. And as we
saw last year, the Treasury is often inclined to talk up the doom and
gloom to open up a positive surprise on the day. 12 months ago,
the hit to headroom was considerably smaller than had been
widely expected.
This time, any positive surprise is most likely to come from wage
growth. Will the rise in energy prices spill into higher pay, just as it
did in 2022? We think it won’t, not least because the jobs market is

much weaker, an assessment we suspect the OBR will share.
But even relatively small upgrades to wage growth can make a big
diﬀerence to the government’s fortunes. Since 2010, the OBR has
revised the level of earnings across its ﬁve-year forecast by an
average of 1.3 percentage points in absolute terms. If it did that
now, say by lifting wage growth over the next two years by 0.6ppt
apiece, it would add £10bn to headroom, almost perfectly
oﬀsetting the hit from higher interest costs.

### Page 4

There are also reasons to think the impact of lower migration
projections, which some reports suggest could wipe another £7bn
oﬀ headroom via lower tax revenue forecasts, could be much less
than feared. The starting point for employment looks a little higher
than it did back in March. The rise in global equities this year should
also be helpful for capital gains tax projections.
Why does this all matter? Because the higher the headroom, the
less ‘need’ there is to hike taxes, and if it is signiﬁcantly higher than

expected, the more room there is to increase spending under the
ﬁscal rules.
The simple message is this: don’t judge the Autumn Budget by the
level of headroom left over.
2. Spending splurge
The problem with ‘headroom’ is not only that it’s highly sensitive to
the OBR’s economic outlook, but that the forward-looking nature of
the ﬁscal rules opens the door to a lot of creative accounting.
The impact of higher inﬂation is a case in point. This helps the

Treasury through higher receipts – both VAT and income tax. Yes, it
also pushes up the cost of indexed beneﬁts. But importantly,
departmental budgets don’t automatically increase to oﬀset the
impact of higher prices.
That opens up a classic trick from the Treasury, which is to leave
spending plans virtually unchanged in nominal terms, setting up
substantially smaller real-terms budget increases across the
medium term.
Even before the Iran War, government plans pencilled in real-terms

cuts to per capita departmental spending in FY2029. Given some
functions, like health, are ‘protected’, that implies much deeper cuts
to smaller departments.
Suﬃce it to say, that is unrealistic. Higher inﬂation will push up the
wage bill across government. It would cost £12bn to simply
maintain those March departmental spending plans in real terms.
That’s before considering the cost of ending that austerity pencilled

### Page 5

in for later this decade, or how the government will fund its defence
or social care ambitions.
A top-up to government departments is inevitable – the question is
when. It looks like the Treasury wants to kick the can on these tricky
budget decisions until next autumn, when it plans to release its
next Spending Review, a process of allocating money across
departments. No doubt the hope is that debt interest costs will
have fallen and the economic outlook brightened, such that there's

more space under the ﬁscal rules to make bigger spending
commitments.
That points to a very modest package of spending measures this
month, mainly targeting the cost of living. On top of the already-
announced cut to VAT on energy, we’d imagine the Treasury will
replicate last year’s budget and lower household bills by funding a
greater share of ‘policy costs’ from general taxation. This has the
added beneﬁt of lowering headline inﬂation at a particularly

sensitive moment for the Bank of England. Crucially though, the
government is likely to bill these moves as temporary, so they don’t
count under that main ﬁscal rule.
But if the economic picture really is better than feared, don’t rule
out the government going further. A decision to fully fund the UK’s
3% defence spending commitment would be a major wildcard,
though this would likely come at the expense of capital plans in
other areas of government.

Ultimately, the key thing to watch is the OBR’s assessment of how
all these policy changes aﬀect GDP growth and borrowing over the
next year. If there’s a material stimulus eﬀect, it could add to
market bets on UK rate hikes.
3. A manifesto break on income tax
Tax hikes are likely at the Autumn Budget, but should be very small.
We’re probably looking at a package of circa £5bn (0.2% of GDP).
Part of the problem is that the list of viable options looks perilously

small if Labour is to avoid breaking its manifesto pledge not to raise

the major taxes, which together account for around 70% of total
revenue.
Our best guess is that the budget will include a hike in the Bank
Surcharge (raising £2bn if increased from 3% to 8%), together with
a broadening of the tax base for National Insurance to landlords
and partners in LLPs (together yielding around £2bn/year). Other
options, like ending a loophole on stamp duty for commercial
property or lowering the VAT threshold for businesses, have also

been on the table.
The problem with many of these measures is that they require
massive changes to stand any chance of raising material sums,
risking big behavioural reactions while oﬀering uncertain revenue
potential. There’s a reason why former chancellor Rachel Reeves
shied away from many of the measures we outline in the chart
below.
How the Chancellor could raise taxes

Is there scope for a surprise? We think there are two main ways on
tax – one good, one bad.
The good? A surprise announcement of a so-called ‘tax switch’, a
novel proposal from Resolution Foundation which would see the
government simultaneously raise basic income tax and cut

employee National Insurance by 2ppt. The impact on workers
would be neutral, but because income tax has a broader base
(including pensions), this would raise £6bn/year.
Though not a substantial sum, it would signal to investors that
Prime Minister Andy Burnham isn’t afraid to go near the thorny
issue of raising taxes. But in light of recent pension changes,

### Page 8

including to the infamous ‘triple lock’, this feels like too much of a
political gamble at this stage.
The negative surprise would be if the Treasury decided to raise the
basic tax allowance. Burnham ﬂirted with this on his ﬁrst day in
oﬃce; it would not only be expensive (potentially £10bn), but it
would also signal that the UK’s commitment to the threshold freeze
– which is doing the lion's share of the planned ﬁscal consolidation
– is waning.
A big rise in the National Living Wage

The minimum wage increase in April 2027, which will be
announced alongside the budget, is perhaps the most important
variable for the Bank of England.
This is based on a recommendation from the Low Pay Commission,
which earlier this year provisionally estimated the increase at 3.7%.
That’s a smaller increase than over the past two years and,
crucially, is not a million miles above the level the Bank considers
consistent with a 2% medium-term inﬂation target (3.25%).

But the LPC has some ﬂexibility in its recommendation, and one
criterion is the cost of living. Higher inﬂation means we could see a
ﬁnal proposal between 4% and 4.5%. Anything above that would,
we suspect, be problematic for the Bank of England and would
bolster the case for higher rates.
The government is no doubt well aware of this. And there appears
to be growing recognition among Labour ﬁgures, including on the
left of the party, that the big minimum wage increases, coupled

with previous payroll tax increases, have had a big impact on
business – particularly in consumer services.
But if the LPC were to recommend minimum wage growth of close
to 5%, could the government resist it? In theory, yes. But politically,
we suspect not.
A surge in bond issuance
We already know that bond issuance is set to increase again in
2027, though for reasons unrelated to the UK’s ﬁscal trajectory.

bills, meaning the actual pick-up in gilt sales relative to those
previous plans is likely to be more modest.

Still, the point is that, beneath these numbers, public sector net
borrowing is still likely to be projected to keep falling through the
rest of the decade. The major risk – related to some of the things

discussed earlier, like larger near-term spending or surprise
increases in tax allowances – would jeopardise this fundamental
improvement in bond issuance in the medium term. That's
something investors wouldn’t take kindly to.

ITEM DEFINITIONS AND METHODOLOGY
3© 2026 LSEG.  All marks herein are used under licence. TRPR_82222_874
General:
All aggregates are bottom-up aggregates calculated from analysts' consensus forecasts unless otherwise noted. Bottom-up
aggregates are weighted calculations using the I/B/E/S data from LSEG share class monitored or the official index weight of
the index-provider. Top-down forecasts are compilations of strategists' estimates. I/B/E/S universe calculations are based on

all companies within a given market monitored by LSEG.
Benchmark index calculations, including those for official local market indices, the S&P 500 Index, and the FTSE World Equity
Indices are calculated using official index constituents and weightings. All index composites are representative of current
index constituents. Index constituents and weightings are updated monthly during options expiration (the Thursday before the
third Friday of every month) for every index except the S&P 500, the constituents of which are current as of the date of

calculation.
PEIGBY refers to the Price Earnings Inverse Government Bond Yield ratio, which measures equity valuation with respect to
the inverse-yield on long-term government bonds (now known as the 'Fed' model). All PEIGBY ratios are I/B/E/S universe-
specific except for the US ratio, where it is based on the S&P 500 Index.
Methodology:
For US and selected Canadian aggregates, calendar-year aggregation is performed using the sum of four quarters' estimates

and a quarterly pro-ration is applied to non-December year end companies' quarterly EPS estimates. Outside of the US and
Canada, all company-level data are calendarized prior to aggregation using the Compustat method of calendarization.
Aggregation is performed using the annual consensus EPS estimate and a May roll-back is applied to December year-end
markets. Aggregates for Japan, where most companies report in March, use an August rollback and a November rollback is

used for Australia and New Zealand, where the fiscal year-end for most companies is in June.
All 12-month-forward data for ex-US markets are calculated using a pro-rata weighting to annual EPS forecasts. The
weightings applied are a daily calibration out of 365 days relative to each constituent's individual fiscal year-end and the
current date.
All non-surprise data, including aggregate forecast earnings, forecast growth, and forward-looking valuation calculations are

weighted according to the I/B/E/S share class monitored for I/B/E/S universes or the weightings assigned to each constituent
in a given index. Aggregated earnings calculations are the equivalent of net profit aggregates, as they are derived from the
EPS forecasts aggregated to the net income level by the appropriate share weighting.
Earnings Aggregation:
For 12-month-forward earnings, the 12-month-forward EPS is used in place of the calendarized EPS in the formulas below.

I/B/E/S universe EPS Aggregate = Index EPS Aggregate =
Growth Calculations:
All growth calculations are the weighted year over year growth in earnings for the calendarized fiscal year period. The
formulas below use index shares for index growth calculations.
Calendarized Earnings Growth = 12-month-forward Growth =
Valuation:
All valuation calculations are the weighted-average price-earnings ratio for the fiscal period in question. The formulas below

use index shares for index P/E calculations.
Calendarized P/E Aggregate = 12-month-forward P/E =
Revisions Momentum:
Unless otherwise indicated, analysts' revisions momentum is a simple aggregation of the total number of estimates raised
divided by the total number of estimates lowered. Thus, any revisions ratio less than 1 indicates negative revisions
momentum while a ratio greater than 1 indicates positive revisions momentum, with 1 marking parity.

The Global Aggregates from LSEG product contains annual aggregates for universes included in this publication, including
supplemental aggregate data of over 100 items as well as history dating back to 1985 for North America and to 1987 for other
markets. To learn more about this product, contact your LSEG sales representative.
FTSE and the FTSE World Equity Indices are owned by FTSE International Limited.

Document 351: Persistent energy pressures have further to run (2026-10-25)

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
MIDDLE EAST
BULLETIN
Persistent energy pressures have further to run
19 October 2026
Key points
• Shipping disruptions, depleted
inventories and elevated freight
costs are keeping physical crude
and fuel markets tight despite a

reported recovery in Gulf exports.
• Brent is forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing
to US$77/bbl in Q4 2027.
• Refined product markets are
expected to ease more slowly than
crude, with elevated gasoil cracks,
shipping and insurance costs slowing
declines in petrol and diesel prices.
• Under an escalation scenario,
renewed conflict and deeper supply
disruptions could push Brent towards
US$150/bbl in H1 2027, prolonging
elevated fuel and LNG prices.

• Under an early resolution scenario, a
durable US-Iran agreement accelerates
supply recovery, taking Brent towards
US$65/bbl by Q4 2027 and bringing
fuel and LNG prices down faster.
Q4 2026 forecasts:
Brent crude
US$101/bbl
Japanese LNG
US$24.5/MMBtu

### Page 2

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Low inventories and higher shipping costs will keep oil and
fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027
as supply recovers. Refined fuels and LNG prices should

ease more slowly. Risks are two-sided: escalation could
push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply with
prices returning close to pre-conflict levels by end-2027.
This article was first published in the October Market Outlook .
Brent crude is back above US$100/bbl on fading confidence
in a lasting US-Iran agreement and intermittent attacks on
shipping. Buffers remain depleted, with the IEA estimating

global observed inventories were 507mb lower at the end of
August than at the start of the conflict.
This comes despite reported crude exports through the Strait of
Hormuz recovering to around 70% to 90% of pre-conflict levels
in late September. Saudi and UAE exports through the Yanbu and
Fujairah pipelines continue, with the East-West pipeline back to
normal after the early September attacks. Tanker crossings have
risen, although transponders remain switched off, leaving visible

transit at 1% to 4% of pre-conflict levels, while cargo transfers in
the Gulf of Oman have increased as an alternative route.
However, these workarounds are slower and more costly. Ship-
to-ship transfers can take 5 to 10 days, US escorts through
the Gulf via a route close to the Oman shoreline cause delays
while some tankers are taking longer routes. Fewer ships are
also available, with many redeployed to other markets. Higher
insurance premiums are adding to costs, with Gulf-to-China

tanker rates above US$0.9mn a day, around 15x higher than a
year ago. With prompt barrels (available for physical delivery)
scarce, the gap between physical and futures prices widened
again, with Dated Brent around US$136/bbl in early October
against US$101/bbl for front-month futures.
Oil and fuel prices push higher ...
Refined prices have risen more than crude
Refined product markets were already tight going into the
conflict. Reduced Gulf supply, exacerbated by Ukrainian strikes

on Russian refineries, has added to that tightness. Together
with higher shipping costs and rising Dated Brent prices,
Singapore Gasoil 10ppm (diesel) has roughly doubled since the
Strait closed, compared with around 40% for crude futures.
Consequently, Singapore gasoil crack spread widened
to an average of around US$36.1/bbl in early October,
from US$17.5/bbl across 2025. Alongside the Australian
government’s excise tax returning to normal, after it had been

halved, this has lifted national diesel and petrol prices to
around A$2.83/L and A$2.41/L.
Outlook
We do not see a lasting US-Iran agreement until at least
mid-2027. In the meantime, we assume there will not be a
return to full-scale military intervention. However, periodic
Iranian attacks on ships and facilities are expected to continue,
as will the US blockade of Iranian exports, keeping a supply
risk premium in place. We look for Brent futures to average

around US$101/bbl in Q4, then ease gradually as demand
from refiners and governments to rebuild stocks partly offsets
recovering supply. As the premium fades and the market
moves into surplus in late 2027, we forecast Brent at around
US$77/bbl in Q4 2027, easing towards US$70/bbl in 2028 as
Middle East production fully recovers.
Oil balance to gradually improve over 2027
Global oil supply is expected to rise by 4.0mb/d (4.0%) to
103.7mb/d in 2027, following a 6.1% fall in 2026. Most of

this reflects a gradual recovery in Gulf output, with Houthi
Sian Fenner, Head of Business & Industry Economics
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Luka Belobajdic, Economist

### Page 3

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
attacks on Red Sea shipping keeping Saudi production below
its pre-conflict level until H2 2027. Meanwhile, we look for UAE
production to continue to improve over 2027 and lift above
pre-conflict levels from 2028, as it is no longer constrained by

OPEC quotas. We do not expect a meaningful return of Iranian
oil until an agreement is reached, and damage sustained during
the conflict means production will take time to recover.
We expect global oil demand to be 103.7mb/d in 2026, 0.8mb/d
(0.7%) higher than in 2025, and to rise a further 1.9mb/d (1.8%)
to 105.6mb/d in 2027. Chinese crude imports will continue to
recover over the coming months as refiners restock ahead of
Lunar New Year, but we expect only a gradual return to pre-

conflict levels, as the electrification of transport limits the
recovery in Chinese demand. Globally, demand for diesel and jet
fuel recovers as transport and aviation activity picks up, although
some demand destruction and efficiency gains are expected
to be permanent. The conflict has also highlighted strategic
vulnerabilities, particularly across Asia, so we expect a push to
rebuild reserves adding around 0.5% to demand over 2027.
Fuel prices to ease more slowly than crude

Refined product supply is expected to recover more slowly
than crude, with Gulf refinery runs not returning to pre-war
levels until at least late 2027. Outside the Gulf, there has
been some constructive news. The US has lifted sanctions
on Russian diesel until April 2027, with Russia saying it will
supply more than 4Mt over the coming months, while China
has lifted its ban on refined product exports. However, we
are cautious about how much this will add to the market in

the short term. Much of the Russian supply is headed to the
US and will depend on Russia’s ability to restore refining and
export capacity, while doubts remain over the Russia-Ukraine
energy ceasefire announced by President Trump. China still
manages exports through quotas, which are likely to increase
only gradually as domestic stocks are rebuilt. Refinery runs
should lift ahead of Lunar New Year, but any increase in
exports will take time to reach regional markets.

With product supply recovering slowly and depleted inventories
being rebuilt, Singapore gasoil crack is likely to stay above its
long-run average even as Brent eases, narrowing from US$71/
bbl in Q4 to US$27/bbl by Q4 2027. With Singapore Gas 10ppm
prices, shipping and insurance costs to remain elevated and
easing only gradually in 2027, we forecast Australian diesel
to retail at an average of 274¢/L in Q4, easing to 200¢/L in Q4
2027, and petrol at 230¢/L and 187¢/L respectively.

LNG prices supported through 2027
Japanese LNG prices surged 15.2%mth in September,
averaging US$25.4/MMBtu as supply disruptions in Qatar
and its inability to divert exports around the Strait of Hormuz
tightened markets and intensified competition between
importers. While prices are now more than double pre-conflict
levels, demand destruction across Asia is providing some
offset to tight supply
Our Q4 forecast for Japanese LNG is now US$24.5/mmbtu and

we expect the market to remain structurally tight, with supply
recovery likely to be gradual and prices not returning to pre-
conflict levels until around 2028. Still, Australia’s domestic
gas market should remain insulated, supported by the FY2027
Federal Budget’s domestic gas reservation scheme, which
requires exporters to supply the domestic market with the
equivalent of up to 20% of gas exports. So far this year,
despite the increase in LNG spot prices in Asia, Australian

natural gas prices have remained under the $12 per GJ cap
that has proposed by the Commonwealth government. We are
expecting this to continue well into 2027.
Risks around our oil price outlook are two-sided
Talks are still ongoing in the background, with the US and
Iran exchanging proposals to reopen the Strait, but the gap
between them remains wide. The conflict has also expanded
to the Red Sea and, with inventories sharply drawn down,
there is a smaller buffer to absorb a further shock.

Using the Oxford Economics model, we assess an escalation
scenario, where energy markets face a deeper and more
persistent disruption as the conflict in the Middle East
intensifies. In this case, Brent futures rise to around US$150/
bbl in H1 2027, only dipping to below US$100/bbl in Q2 2028.
We also consider an early resolution scenario, where shipping
and Gulf supply normalise faster, returning Brent to near pre-
conflict levels by end-2027.
Escalation scenario

In the escalation scenario we assume:
Talks stall and the conflict escalates:  the gap between the
US and Iran proves too wide and the US resumes strikes after
the midterms. Iran responds with attacks on shipping and Gulf
energy infrastructure, damaging export, storage and refining
facilities. A lasting agreement is not reached until 2028.
Export routes are disrupted: more frequent attacks curtail
dark crossings of the Strait and ship-to-ship transfers in the

Gulf of Oman. Houthi attacks also cut off Saudi alternative
routes, forcing Gulf producers to cut output and Middle
East crude exports sharply lower. New routes and shipping
arrangements eventually support a recovery in Gulf flows, but
trade patterns take time to adjust.
... and risks remain and while they are two sided ...

4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Product supply fails to recover:  The Russia–Ukraine energy
ceasefire does not hold and strikes on Russian refineries
continue, limiting how much diesel Russia can export,
even with the US sanctions waiver extended. China also

reintroduces its export ban to protect domestic supply.
Oil prices lift to around US$150/bbl in H1 2027: With little
buffer to absorb the supply loss, competition for physical
delivery intensifies, with physical prices well above futures
as prompt barrels remain scarce. Higher prices support
supply growth outside OPEC and drive demand destruction,
alongside further emergency stock releases, but a deeper
and more prolonged global deficit remains. Brent stays above

US$100/bbl until mid-2028 as governments rebuild reserves.
The Singapore gasoil crack widens and, with freight and
insurance costs also higher, Australian diesel and petrol prices
average around 247 cpl and 231 cpl at end 2027. Japanese
LNG also remains elevated around US$23.2/MMBtu in Q4
2027, $9.1 above our baseline.
Early resolution scenario
In the early resolution scenario we assume:
A durable agreement: The US and Iran agree terms to reopen
the Strait by the end of year, and, unlike the June interim deal,

the agreement holds, with progress towards a permanent
settlement in early 2027. Attacks on shipping cease and the
US blockade is lifted, allowing Iranian oil back into the market,
initially from storage. Vessels are quickly repositioned to the
Gulf, with freight and war-risk insurance easing from early
2027 rather than mid-year.
A faster recovery in supply:  With export routes secure, Gulf
production and damaged refinery and export capacity recover

sooner than in the baseline. Outside the Gulf, the US extends
its waiver on Russian diesel beyond April 2027 and China
raises export quotas as domestic stocks are rebuilt.
Demand settles below pre-conflict trend: Some demand
destruction and efficiency gains persist, particularly in China.
With inventories rebuilding faster, government restocking is
smaller and more gradual than in the baseline.
Prices fall quickly, but restocking provides a floor:  Brent

Brent futures ease to around US$88/bbl in Q1 2027 as the
premium for prompt physical barrels unwinds. As the market
moves into balance, Brent eases to around US$65/bbl by Q4
2027, close to pre-conflict levels.
Refined product prices ease faster on lower freight costs with
the Singapore gasoil crack narrowing. Australian diesel and
petrol prices fall to around 182 cpl and 170 cpl by Q4 2027. Gas
markets also ease, although damage to Qatar’s gas facility means

export capacity recovers gradually. Japanese LNG falls to around
US$11.9/MMBtu by end-2027, $2.1 below our baseline.
Economic impact
In the escalation scenario, higher fuel costs flow through to
transport, food and other energy-intensive goods such as
plastics and fertilisers. Australian headline CPI remains above
4.5% for much of 2027, with underlying inflation remaining
sticky at above 3% until late 2028. The RBA faces a difficult
trade off between much higher inflation and a significant

hit to activity, as households cut discretionary spending and
businesses defer investment. Higher LNG export earnings
provide only a moderate offset. In the early resolution scenario,
headline inflation is around 0.1ppt below the baseline in 2027.
New Zealand is more exposed in both scenarios, with food
and energy a larger share of its CPI and no offsetting energy
exports. Headline inflation is around 1ppt higher in the
escalation scenario in Q1 2027.

... the upside presents a more dramatic shift in prices

Asia Trends

1/5 
インド中銀、３年８ヵ月ぶりの利上げ､政策スタンスも転換 
～インフレを警戒した「予防的引き締め」、追加利上げに含みも慎重姿勢、ルピーや株価は？～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ インド準備銀行（ＲＢＩ）は 10 月の定例会合で、政策金利を25bp 引き上げて5.50％とし、
2023 年２月以来３年８ヵ月ぶりの利上げに踏み切った。政策スタンスも「中立」から「調整
された引き締め」に変更し、追加利上げの可能性を残した。これまでＲＢＩは景気に配慮し
て、利上げを回避しつつ、ルピー安への対応を進めてきた。しかし、資金流入策に伴う銀行
システムの過剰流動性という副作用も生じ、 金融市場では予防的利上げ観測が強まっていた。 
○ インフレを巡っては、原油高の一服にもかかわらず卸売物価が高止まりしており、消費者物
価もＲＢＩの目標レンジ内ながら中央値を上回っている。加えて、モンスーンの雨量不足や
エルニーニョ現象、肥料価格の高止まりなどを背景に、先行きは食料品を中心に物価上昇が
長期化する懸念がある。このためＲＢＩは、景気が堅調なうちに物価上昇を抑える「予防的
利上げ」に踏み切ったとみられる。 
○ ＲＢＩは 2026-27 年度の経済成長率見通しを＋7.1％に上方修正する一方、インフレ率見通
しも＋5.2％に引き上げた。 先行きの政策について、 利下げの可能性を否定し、 選択肢を 「利
上げ」 か 「据え置き」 に限定したものの、 追加利上げの時期や規模については明言を避けた。 
○ このため、ＲＢＩは引き締め方向に転じたが、連続的な利上げには慎重とみられる。政策の
先行きが読みづらい状況が続くなか、ルピー相場は上値の重い展開が見込まれるほか、ＲＢ
Ｉによる資金吸収策も重なり、株価（ムンバイSENSEX）も上値を抑えられる可能性がある。

### Page 2

2/5 
【RBIは３年８ヵ月ぶりの利上げ決定に加え、政策スタンスの変更で追加利上げに含み】 
インド準備銀行（ＲＢＩ）は、10 月５
～７日 に開催し た定例の金融政策委員
会において、 政策金利であるレポ金利を
25bp 引き上げて 5.50％とすることを決
定した（図１）。ＲＢＩによる利上げ決定
は 2023 年２月以来であり、３年８ヵ月
ぶりである。さらに、今回は政策スタン
スも中立（neutral）から 「調整された引
き締め（calibrated tightening）」に変
更しており、先行きの追加利上げに含み
を持たせた格好である。 
ＲＢＩは、８月の前回会合において、全会一致で金利を据え置く様子見姿勢を維持した。 しかし、
その後に公表された議事要旨では、 政策委員の間で先行きの政策運営に関する温度差が確認された
（注１）。さらに、 ＲＢＩは景気に配慮して利上げを回避する一方、 金融市場でのルピー安が輸入イ
ンフレを招く懸念が高まった。このため、ＲＢＩは６月会合で、非居住インド人に対する優遇ドル
預金制度、企業・金融機関への対外商業借入のヘッジコスト補助導入など、ルピー相場の支援策を
発表した。 政府も、 適格外国人投資家による国債売却益へのキャピタルゲイン税撤廃を発表するな
ど、海外からの資金流入を促すことでルピー相場を下支えする方針を明らかにした。 
その後、海外からの資金流入が活発化して外貨準備高は積み上がった。一方で、国内銀行にはド
ル建て預金が急拡大し、各行はＲＢＩとのスワップを通じてコストなしでルピーに交換し たため、
銀行システムに過剰流動性が発生した。このため、ＲＢＩは過剰流動性によるインフレ増幅を警戒
して、資金吸収オペ（売りオペ）を活発化したほか、為替スワップを通じてルピー資金の吸収を図
る事態に追い込まれた （注２） 。 このように、 ＲＢＩが利上げを回避しつつ、 ルピー相場の安定を目
指したことによる「副作用」に直面してきた。このため、金融市場においては、ＲＢＩが早晩「予
防的利上げ」に舵を切るとの見方が強まっていた。

注１ ８月20 日付レポート「インド中銀､政策委員の間で将来的な利上げ可能性の認識が広がる」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/654396.html） 
注２ ９月10 日付レポート「ルピー防衛の「副作用」に直面するインド中銀」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/654982.html）

### Page 3

3/5 
【インフレのさらなる上振れが懸念されるなかで「予防的利上げ」に舵】 
インドは原油需要の約９割を輸入に依
存しており、 イラン情勢による原油高は
対外収支の悪化に加え、 物価上昇を招く
ことが懸念された。 米国とイランによる
停戦合意を受けて原油高が一服し、 エネ
ルギー価格の上昇圧力が後退している
ものの、８月の卸売物価上昇率は前年同
月比＋9.92％と 10％近くで推移するな
ど高止まりしている （図２） 。このため、
川上段階においてはインフレが顕在化
するとともに、 長期化している様子がう
かがえる。 
インドでは、 米国の関税引き上げによ
る悪影響の緩和を目的に、2025 年９月に
ＧＳＴ（財・サービス税）の実質引き下
げに動いたため、 その後のインフレ率は
下振れした。 さらに、 モディ政権は国民
にパニック買いを控えて節約を呼び掛
けつつ、 景気に配慮してガソリン・軽油
価格を据え置いた。一方で、 国営石油公
社は損失を吸収して供給を維持したた
め、 金融市場ではこうした対応が財政悪
化を招くとの懸念が広がり、 ルピー相場
は調整の動きを強めて輸入インフレを招くことが警戒された。さらに、資金流出に加え、ＲＢＩが
ルピー相場の防衛を目的とする断続的な為替介入に動いたことも重なり、 外貨準備高が急減すると
の懸念も広がりをみせた。このため、モディ政権は５月、国民に在宅勤務の励行や公共交通機関の
活用による燃料需要抑制、 海外渡航や金購入の自粛による外貨準備の防衛を呼び掛けた（注３）。そ
のうえ、ガソリン・軽油価格を段階的に引き上げるなど政策転換を図った。その後のインフレ率は
加速の動きを強めており、 直近８月は前年同月比＋4.82％とＲＢＩの定める目標レンジ （４±２％）
の域内で推移しているものの、中央値を上回るなどインフレが警戒される水準にある（図３） 。 
先行きについても、 イラン情勢は不透明な状況が続いており、 エネルギー価格の高止まりを招く
可能性は高い。 そのうえ、2026 年のモンスーン （雨季） の雨量は、 インド全体では長期平均を約15％

注３ ５月12 日付レポート「インド･モディ首相､在宅勤務など燃料需要抑制策を呼び掛け」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/606977.html）

### Page 4

4/5 
下回り、2009 年以来 17 年ぶりの低水準となり、カリフ（雨季作）への悪影響が懸念される。さら
に、エルニーニョ現象の発生による少雨・干ばつも予想され、播種の遅れや作付面積の減少が農業
生産を下押しする可能性も高まっている。 イラン情勢の悪化による化学肥料価格の高止まりも重な
り、 農業生産の低迷が食料インフレを招くことも考えられ、 生活必需品を中心にインフレが長引く
ことが警戒される。このため、ＲＢＩは「予防的利上げ」に向けた重い腰を上げた格好である。

【声明文は先行きの「利上げ」か「現状維持」とするも、その行方は明言を避ける】 
会合後に公表した声明文では、 ６人の政策委員が全会一致で政策金利の引き上げを決定する一方、
政策スタンスの変更については４人が賛成（２人が反対）したことを明らかにした。反対意見を表
明したのは、クマール委員（工業開発研究所（ISID）所長兼ＣＥＯ）とシン委員（デリー経済学院
（ＤＳＥ）学長）であり、２人は中立を維持すべきと表明したことを明らかにしている。 
世界経済については 「８月会合以降、 イラン情勢の悪化と原油価格の変動を理由に不安定化して
いる」としつつ、 「世界経済のモメンタムは依然として強靱」との見方を示した。そのうえで、 「主
要国でのインフレ加速が金融政策をタカ派にシフトさせている 」ほか、 「国際金融市場の環境変化
と主要国の財政持続可能性への懸念が重なり、 金利は高止まりしている」 とした。 ただし、 「イラン
情勢を巡る不透明感、国際金融市場の引き締め環境、ＡＩ（人工知能）関連資産の高止まり、公的
債務水準の高さが下振れリスクを引き起こす可能性は残る」との認識を示した。 
一方で、同国経済は「世界経済を巡る逆風にもかかわらず強靱さを維持している」 、 「個人消費や
投資、輸出の回復が景気を下支えしている」 、 「製造業が供給サイドをけん引している」との見方を
示した。そして、足元の同国経済も「経済活動が継続的に拡大している」としつつ、先行きは「世
界経済の不確実性が影響を与えることが懸念される」とした。そのうえで、2026-27 年度の経済成
長率見通しを 「＋7.1％」 と従来見通し （＋6.7％） から上方修正したうえで、 「リスクは上下双方に
均衡している」との見方を示した。 
物価動向については 「足元の上昇は食料品やエネルギーの上昇による」 としつつ、 「コアインフレ
率も上昇するなど、 物価上昇圧力が広がりをみせている」 との認識を示した。 そして、 「短期的には
モンスーンの雨量不足、 エルニーニョ現象の影響、 エネルギーをはじめとする商品市況の高騰など
供給サイドからの上昇圧力が続く」との見通しを示している。そのうえで、2026-27 年度のインフ
レ率見通しを 「＋5.2％」 と従来見通し （＋5.0％） から上方修正したうえで、 「リスクは上下双方に
均衡している」との見方を示した。 
今回の決定について「入手可能なデータに照らせば、物価見通しは 2025 年のように良好ではな
い」 ことを理由に 「政策金利の微調整が避けられない」 との認識を示した。 一方で、 「需要サイドの
インフレ圧力は限定的」であるものの、 「市場に流通するマネーや銀行貸出の拡大によるインフレ
リスクは残る」 との見方を示した。 このため、 「利上げに加え、 政策スタンスを変更する」 とし、 先
行きについて「利下げの可能性は完全になくなり、今後の選択肢は利上げか現状維持（据え置き）
になる」との考えを示した。そのうえで、利上げサイクルの期間と規模については「景気動向や物
価動向・見通しに左右される」として明言を避けた。

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5

【今後も慎重姿勢を維持する可能性、ルピー相場や主要株価指数は上値の重い展開が続くか】 
会合後に記者会見に臨んだＲＢＩのマルホトラ総裁は、足元の同国経済について「 堅調であり、
成長の勢いは引き続き幅広い分野に及んでいる」との認識を示した。しかし、前回会合以降のイン
フレ見通しが悪化したとして、今回の利上げ実施を決定したことを明らかにした。そのうえで、物
価動向について「インフレ率は今後３四半期に平均で＋5.8％程度への加速が予想される」とし、
「こうした状況では政策金利の調整が避けられない」との見通しを示した。 
とはいえ、 声明文やマルホトラ総裁の記者会見の内容からは、 ＲＢＩは今回利上げに踏み切った
ものの、なお慎重姿勢を維持しており、
断続的な利上げには距離を置く姿勢が
うかがえる。こうしたことから、 金融市
場においてはＲＢＩによる「次の一手」
が読みにくいとの見方が広がると予想
される。 よって、 ＲＢＩは利上げを決定
したにもかかわらず、 ルピー相場は上値
の重い展開が続くと予想される （図４） 。
一方で、 金融市場における過剰流動性が
懸念されるなかで、 ＲＢＩは資金吸収オ
ペを活発化させていることも重なり、 主
要株価指数（ムンバイSENSEX）も同様に上値の重い展開が続く可能性に要注意である。

Document 524: 从CoreWeave到Anthropic，芯片抵押融资正重塑AI债务市场

Source PDF: all report pdf/从CoreWeave到Anthropic，芯片抵押融资正重塑AI债务市场.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

芯⽚抵押融资始于 2023 年⿊⽯向 CoreWeave 提供的⼀笔 23 亿美元私募信贷，如今博通正
与华尔街机构磋商为 Anthropic 安排 600 亿美元芯⽚融资， SpaceX 据报正寻求为英伟达芯
⽚融资 400 亿美元，英伟达本⾝亦宣布与六⼤贷款机构达成 5000 亿美元合作框架。这⼀新
兴资产类别规模或突破万亿美元， AI 商业化能否兑现预期是关键。
摘要：
以芯⽚为抵押物的结构性融资，正在成为全球 AI 扩张的核⼼资⾦引擎，并迅速发展为⼀个规模可
能突破万亿美元的全新资产类别。
这⼀市场的雏形始于 2023 年⿊⽯向 CoreWeave 提供的⼀笔 23 亿美元私募信贷，成为芯⽚抵押债
务的⾸单标志性交易。
如今，博通正与华尔街机构磋商为 Anthropic 安排 600 亿美元芯⽚融资， SpaceX 据报正寻求为
英伟达芯⽚融资 400 亿美元，英伟达本⾝亦宣布与六⼤贷款机构达成 5000 亿美元合作框架。
摩根⼤通分析师预计， 2030 年前芯⽚⽀出总额或超 3 万亿美元，其中⼤部分将依赖举债融资。

然⽽，这场债务扩张也带来不容忽视的⻛险。由于交易多为私募性质，投资者缺乏便捷的退出
渠道；芯⽚⼚商、云计算巨头与新兴云服务商之间的交叉敞⼝⾼度集中。
CoreWeave 企业层⾯的垃圾级债券近期⼤幅下挫，部分跌⾄⾯值 80 美分，收益率攀升⾄约
15% ，折射出市场对 AI 商业回报的深层疑虑。
⼀笔融资开创新范式
这场⾰命的起点，是⿊⽯战术机遇团队对⼀个陌⽣概念的审慎评估。据当时参与尽调的
CoreWeave 联合创始⼈ Brannin McBee 回忆，⿊⽯⾼管当年曾直接发问： " 什么是 GPU ？ ""AI 是
真实存在的吗？ "
获得满意答案后，⿊⽯于 2023 年主导完成了对 CoreWeave 的 23 亿美元私募信贷，开创了芯⽚抵
押债务融资的先例。
这⼀结构的核⼼逻辑在于：成⽴特殊⽬的载体（ SPV ）购买并持有芯⽚，以⽬标客⼾合同产⽣
的现⾦流偿还债务，债务期限与芯⽚五⾄六年的折旧周期相匹配，从⽽将硬件贬值⻛险锁定在
可预测范围之内。
CoreWeave 的模式脱胎于商品市场经验 —— 类似液化天然⽓领域 " 先签合同、再融资建⼚ " 的开

发模式。⾸笔交易以微软合同为抵押，彼时融资成本⾼达中双位数利率⽔平， McBee 坦⾔这是
他 " 职业⽣涯中最艰难的⼀次尽调 " 。
随着 CoreWeave 客⼾阵容持续扩⼤，其融资成本随之下降。今年 3 ⽉，公司以 Meta 合同为⽀撑
完成 85 亿美元债务融资，借款利率降⾄约 6%—— 华尔街按照 Meta 的⾼评级⽽⾮ CoreWeave ⾃
⾝的垃圾评级来定价⻛险。
芯⽚成本远超数据中⼼
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

理解这场融资⾰命的规模，需要⾸先理解芯⽚⽀出的量级。芯⽚造价是容纳它们的数据中⼼的
两⾄四倍，却在五⾄六年内折旧殆尽，由此形成了传统资本市场难以独⽴消化的巨额融资缺
⼝。
以 AI 云服务商 Nscale 在西弗吉尼亚州的旗舰项⽬为例，配套发电⼚预计耗资 65 亿美元，数据中
⼼及设备造价 180 亿美元，⽽ GPU 采购成本则⾼达 470 亿美元。
Alphabet 旗下⾕歌、微软、亚⻢逊、 Meta 等投资级云计算巨头可借助充裕现⾦或廉价债券融资
覆盖上述硬件成本，但 OpenAI 、 Anthropic 及⼀众新兴云服务商并不具备这样的资产负债表实
⼒。
OpenAI 业务财务官 Mike Liberatore 坦⾔： " 基础设施建设规模史⽆前例。没有任何剧本告诉你，
如何在极度资本密集的情况下，⽤传统⽅式从零⾛向通⽤⼈⼯智能。 "
为此，业界移植了项⽬融资与结构性融资的机制：通过 SPV 将每笔交易⻛险隔离，以交易本⾝
的质量和客⼾信⽤⽽⾮发⾏⼈⾃⾝评级为基础举债，往往不计⼊企业资产负债表。

芯⽚⼚商直接⼊场背书
博通与英伟达的深度介⼊，将这⼀市场推向了新的量级。两家公司为相关债务交易提供 " 兜底 " 承
诺：
⼀旦借款⽅违约，芯⽚将被重新租赁或出售以偿还剩余债务，芯⽚⼚商负责填补缺⼝。这⼀机
制使 SPV 发⾏的优先级债券能够与芯⽚⼚商的投资级评级挂钩，显著压低融资成本。
Apollo 与⿊⽯近期联合完成了⼀笔 350 亿美元的 "Big Sky" 交易，为 Anthropic 提供⽀持。这也是博
通、 Apollo 与⿊⽯联合搭建的 "AI XPV" 平台⾸单落地交易 —— 美国银⾏策略师估算，该平台若实
现 20 吉⽡的融资容量，⾄ 2029 年中期或需 2350 亿美元有背书债务。
在此次 Anthropic 交易中，博通提供兜底⽀持的两只优先级债券获得投资级评级，最⼤⼀档利率
为 5.75% ；⻛险较⾼的 45 亿美元次级档定价 8.5%—— 仍远低于 Anthropic 作为未评级私营公司的
独⽴融资成本。
今年 8 ⽉，英伟达更集结了 Apollo 、⿊⽯、⻉莱德、 Brookfield 、 KKR 及⾼盛，共同为 AI 基础设施

提供融资来源，英伟达本⾝承诺兜底其中最⾼ 25% 的份额。
从私募⾛向银团市场
要实现真正意义上的规模化，芯⽚融资必须突破私募市场的边界，进⼊银团贷款乃⾄投资级公
开债券市场 —— 后者是全球规模最⼤、成本最低的资本池之⼀。
CoreWeave 今年率先完成了芯⽚⽀持的银团杠杆贷款，两笔交易均获机构投资者稳健认购，在
市场上交易价格接近⾯值，与同⼀公司的企业级债券形成鲜明对⽐。
摩根⼠丹利结构性产品与私募资本市场全球负责⼈ Rich Myers 表⽰： " 这些融资与市场上最具价
值的资产直接挂钩，也就是芯⽚所产⽣的算⼒。⼤量证据表明，算⼒需求将持续增⻓，且超过
算⼒供应。 "
Apollo 全球资产配置负责⼈ Chris Edson 则表⽰，传统公开债务市场⽆法独⼒满⾜数字基础设施
的融资规模，需要项⽬融资、私募配售、保险资⾦及商业房地产等多类资本参与。获得投资级
评级，是打开保险资⾦等最⼤资本池的关键钥匙。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
⻛险积聚不容忽视
然⽽，随着资本加速涌⼊，⻛险也在同步积聚。
Fitch Ratings ⾼级董事 Jason Pompeii 指出，当前的⼚商融资规模前所未有，且⻛险⾼度集
中。 " 这只会增加⼀种可能性：⼀旦 AI 最终令⼈失望，整条供应链都将受到冲击。 "
交易结构的私募性质导致流动性存疑。由于⼤多数芯⽚融资交易不在公开市场流通，投资者难
以便捷退出，⽽交易将少数芯⽚⼚商、云计算巨头与新兴云服务商深度绑定，交叉敞⼝⾼度集
中。 Magnetar 替代信贷与固定收益业务董事总经理 Dave Snyderman 警告： " 巨头只会更⼤。只
有他们才能获得融资渠道。 "
客⼾合同的法律效⼒同样存在疑问。 Carlyle 资产⽀持融资负责⼈ Akhil Bansal 直⾔： " 所有⼈都
假设这些合同牢不可破，但我关注的是现实层⾯ —— 如果只有七家机构为 GPU 提供所有信⽤⽀

持，筹码究竟在谁⼿⾥？ "
Nuveen 私募固定收益负责⼈ Laura Parrott 则表⽰，她⽬前暂缓参与⼤型投资级芯⽚交易，待市
场结构更为成熟后再⾏评估。 " 这是⼀种全新结构，⼀旦出现某种冲击，我们尚不清楚结果会如
何演变。 "
此外，宏观层⾯的变数同样存在：德克萨斯州的地⽅反对势⼒已促成新项⽬暂停令，甲⻣⽂在
新墨西哥州的项⽬受监管阻碍被迫推迟付款， OpenAI 与 Anthropic 也在就安全问题讨论放缓部署
节奏。
迈向 " 算⼒资产类别 "
尽管挑战并存，市场参与者对这⼀范式的⻓期潜⼒仍持乐观态度。
⿊⽯战术机遇团队⾼级董事总经理 John Watson 将当前时刻⽐作汽⻋⾏业的早期岁⽉：当年为福
特 T 型⻋提供融资的⼈同样⾯临 " ⼈们真的会开⻋吗 " 的疑问，如今汽⻋⾦融已发展为规模庞⼤、
运作成熟的市场。
在芯⽚市场演进层⾯，早期研究与实际使⽤数据显⽰，芯⽚的实际使⽤寿命正超出当前会计处
理所体现的年限，这将改善其可融资性。美国银⾏基础设施与可持续融资全球负责⼈ Karen

Fang 预计，类似⼆⼿⻋价格指南的半导体估值⼯具将逐步出现，进⽽催⽣更活跃的芯⽚租赁与
对冲市场。
Watson 描绘的终极愿景是：将⼀个算⼒单位转化为可在全球范围内交易和融资的资产类别
——" 我们理解算⼒的经济价值，因⽽为它融资。 "
实现这⼀⽬标，仍有赖于 AI 商业化进程真正兑现市场预期。

台北富邦銀行 
Oct 5，2026 | 3:00 PM (GMT+8) 
債券市場總評： 非投等高息有撐
投資研究部 
 Research 
Financial Market 
▎美國經濟具韌性，支撐年底升息預期 
9 月下旬以來，美債殖利率快速上行，主要因經濟數
據仍具韌性，加上中東情勢反覆推升油價與通膨疑
慮，牽動市場持續評估後續升息空間。雖然 8 月 PCE
與核心 PCE 低於預期，但先前公布的 9 月製造業與服
務業 PMI 大幅優於預期，第 2 季 GDP 終值上修，顯
示經濟動能仍強。但最新 9 月非農意外疲弱，新增就
業僅 2.9 萬人、失業率升至 4.2%，降低短期升息急迫
性，10 月升息機率降溫，但 12 月升息預期仍存，市
場轉向觀望 9 月 CPI 公布，10 年期美債殖利率短暫回
落後仍處高檔。 
圖 1 就業數據降溫，惟美債殖利率仍處高檔

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/5 
▎殖利率攀升至高位，債市同步下滑 
近日部分 Fed 官員表示，後續利率決策仍需觀察更多
數據，略為緩和市場對進一步升息的定價，帶動 10 年
期美債殖利率自高點小幅回落至 5.25%。不過，殖利
率與油價仍處高檔，限制債市修復空間。9 月下旬以
來，主要債券 YTD 報酬同步下滑，美國公債與投資級
債跌幅較深，非投等債則因存續期較短、風險偏好尚
穩，跌幅相對較輕。

圖 2 利率與油價高檔，主要債券報酬下滑

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/5 
◼ 近期美國數據仍具韌性 ， 中東局勢推升油價與通膨疑慮 ，PCE 雖低於預期 ，但 PMI
與 GDP 仍強，非農轉弱緩和升息預期，美債殖利率仍處高檔，債市承壓。美非投
等債受高息收與中短存續期緩衝 ， 跌幅較輕 ， 殖利率升至8.3%， 處近10 年高位。 
◼ 美非投等債信用結構較過去改善，較高評級的 BB 級占比已逾半，違約率約 2%，
位於近 25 年低檔。美非投等企業 2Q 營收與 EBITDA 分別年增 10.4%、14.4%，
增速加快並創近 4 年較強表現 ， 獲利增速快於營收 ， 也帶動槓桿與償息指標改善。 
◼ 殖利率高檔震盪下，債券報酬較難仰賴價格貢獻，息收成為更重要的收益來源。
美非投等債因票息較高、存續期較短，可提供一定報酬緩衝。後續可依風險承受
度，關注科技、金屬與礦業、能源等基本面改善較明顯產業，並偏向 BB、短中天
期及現金流較穩定發行公司。

美國非投等債報酬率 0.14% 
美國投等債報酬率 -2.74% 
美國公債報酬率 -3.13%

美國 10 年公債殖利率 5.25% 
西德州原油期貨 $90/桶 
美國 10 年期公債殖利率 5.25% 
美國基準利率 3.75~4% 
9 月非農就業新增 2.9 萬人 
9 月失業率 4.2% 
8 月 PCE 年增率 3.4% 
8 月核心 PCE 年增率 3%

▎高息收提供緩衝，支撐非投等債韌性 
近期公債殖利率快速上行 ， 推升企業債殖利率至近年相
對高位，其中美國非投資等級債到期殖利率已來到約
8.3%，顯著高於過去 10 年平均，殖利率水準已具備相
當吸引力。 
圖 3 美非投等債殖利率攀升至 8.3%，10 年相對高檔

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/1

觀察近 6 個月各類債券報酬來源 ， 今年美國公債殖利率
攀升，使債券報酬較難仰賴價格貢獻，息收重新成為影
響總報酬的關鍵來源。其中，美國非投資等級債因具備
較高殖利率與中短存續期，且息收水準相對較高，較能
緩衝利率上行對價格的影響， 因此相較公債與投等債，
回落幅度相對有限。

圖 4 利息收益貢獻為債券報酬主要來源

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/30

▎美經濟相對穩健 ， 非投等BB 比重提升 
從已開發國家 GDP 年增率來看，美國、歐元區與日本
未來幾季仍維持正成長， 其中美國成長表現相對穩健 ，
預估仍維持約 2%左右增速 ， 顯示高利率與油價波動下 ，
經濟仍具一定韌性。

圖 5 美國經濟動能仍相對歐元區、日本較具韌性

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/26

信用結構方面， 美國非投等債市場債信結構已較金融海
嘯前改善，BB 評級占比由 2007 年 12 月的 32%升至
2026 年 5 月的 54%，目前占整體非投等債比重過半，
CCC 與未評級占比則由 15%降至 7%。 由於BB 是非投
等債中評級最高的一層，信用風險低於 B 與 CCC，BB
級占比提高代表目前美國非投等債市場中 ，信用體質較
接近投資等級的發行公司占比明顯提升。 
圖 6 美國非投等債各信用評級占比與違約率

資料來源：JPM，2026/8/31

過去 6 個月，債券報酬組成因子

美國非投等債殖利率 8.35% 
美國投等債殖利率 6.01%

3

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
違約率方面，目前美國非投等債整體違約率約 2%，落
在歷史區間相對低位 ， 低評級債違約壓力也未見明顯擴
散。在美國經濟仍具韌性、非投等債 BB 比重提升且違
約率受控的背景下 ， 美國非投等債的高殖利率仍具基本
面支撐。

▎美非投等企業，2Q 營收獲利顯著改善 
檢視美國非投等債企業第 2 季基本面，營收與 EBITDA
延續正成長動能，且2Q26 成長幅度進一步加速。美國
非投等債企業營收季增 9.2%、 年增10.4%，EBITDA 季
增 19.2%、 年增14.4%， 營收與EBITDA 年增率雙雙創
下 4 年來最高，顯示企業營運表現仍具韌性。

圖 7  美非投等企業營收年增 10.4%，4 年來最快增速

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15

從產業表現來看，今年商品、能源與企業投資需求仍延
續，金屬與礦業、能源、科技、零售與服務業等產業對
整體營收與 EBITDA 成長形成貢獻，使 2Q 基本面改善
具備一定廣度。EBITDA 增速高於營收增速，代表企業
獲利擴張速度快於收入成長，反映成本控管、營運效率
或價格轉嫁能力仍在。 
圖 8 EBITDA 年增顯著提升至 14%，4 年來較強增速

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15 
▎美非投等槓桿回落，償息能力改善 
2Q 美國非投等債企業信用指標出現改善，槓桿率降至 
4.51 倍 ， 主要反映EBITDA 成長速度快於債務增加 ， 帶
動槓桿指標修復。

圖 9 美非投等企業槓桿率回落至 4.51 倍

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15

償息能力方面，儘管利息費用仍在增加，但 EBITDA 成
長仍帶動利息保障倍數回升至 4.12 倍，2 年較高水準 ，
顯示企業獲利對利息支出的覆蓋能力有所改善 ， 高利率
環境下，美非投等企業的償息能力仍相對有撐。整體來
看，2Q26 美國非投等債企業營收、獲利與信用指標同
步改善，基本面韌性仍在。

圖 10 美非投等企業利息保障倍數升至 2 年高位

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15

### Page 4

4

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
▎非投等具高息收優勢 ， 關注BB 短天期 
近期公債殖利率仍在高檔波動 ， 美非投等債短線表現受
到壓抑。不過，經過利率重新定價後，美非投等債殖利
率已回到相對具吸引力的水準 ， 較高息收與相對較短存
續期，仍可在利率波動環境下提供一定報酬緩衝。 
綜合前述，美國經濟相對歐日仍較穩健，企業營收、獲
利與信用指標同步改善 ， 為美非投等債後續表現提供基
本面基礎。產業面可留意科技、基礎工業相關的金屬與
礦業、能源等基本面改善較明顯的方向。整體而言，後
續可偏向 BB、短中天期及現金流較穩定的發行公司，
參與較高息收帶來的收益機會。

動態更新  軟體  ‧美國

Amazon 
(AMZN.O/AMZN US)

3Q26 財報前瞻：AWS 持續加速成長，惟買方投資人預期已堆高 
重要訊息  
Amazo n 預計於台灣時間 10/30 凌晨公 布 3Q26 財報。 
評論及分析  
Holiday  Season 線上銷售 年增率 預期持平。根據 Adobe  Analytics 預估美
國 2026 購物旺季(11 -12 月)線上銷售額將達 US$275bn 年增 6.7% vs. 2025
年增 6.8% 大致持平，係來自於全品項折扣幅度約 18-30%與 2025 相當，AI
連結導購至網站年增 130% 。4M26 起徵收 3.5% 燃油及物流附加費，禁止
Meta Muse 或未與公司充分 溝通的 Agent 服務取得 網站資料，Amazon Ads
與 ChatGPT 試行對話式廣告投放，皆為應對高油價及 AI 變革之措施。我們
正向看待電商業務對 3Q26 財報/4Q26 財測為正向助益 。 
AWS 持續加速 成長 ，惟 買方投資人 預期已堆高 。 根據 ： (1) 媒體揭露
Anthropic  2Q/3Q26 營收 US$11.6/16.8bn ，我們以推論 +訓練算力及通路費
用加總佔比營收 40-50%計算，AWS 為 Anthropic 主要算力供應商及銷售渠
道(佔比預估 60%)； (2)GPT -6 獲得正向回饋並已 在 Bedrock 上架，OpenAI
與 AWS 合作逐漸緊密，惟 2GW Trn 採用將於 2027 上線；(3)標準化算力價
格 持續回升尤其在 Blackwell 系 列更為明顯， EC2 部分服務於 7/1 調漲
20% ，10/7 將再調漲 15%；(4)管理層股東信指出 AWS 若直接外售 自研晶
片或硬體系統將增加 US$30bn 潛在營收。 我們預期 AWS 營收年增率於
2H26-2027 持續加速成長並優於賣方市場共識，惟我們觀察到買方投資人對
2H26 成長預期已達 40%+ 與賣方市場共識出現落差。  
資本支出 同時支 應 AI Labs  / Enterprise  / Rufus 成長 。管理層先前指出
4Q25/2025 新增算力 1.2/3.9GW ，2027 年底算力容量翻倍，我們估算
2027 AWS Trn/NVDA VR 每 GW 建置成本 US$25- 30bn/US$55 -60bn ，AWS
每 GW 平均建置成本約 US$40-45bn 下緣，AWS 資本支出佔總資本支出
90%(其於為物流基礎設施 )，我們預期 2027 資本支出 US$300-330bn，故
市場共識將再上修約 11%，對 FCF 而言為龐大負擔。  
投資建議  
我們維持 Amazon 「增加持股」建議 並調升 目標價 340 美元，係以 2027 年
GAAP 每股盈餘 32 倍換算 。短期 Muse 雜音/ 回檔將創造長線布局良機。  
投資風險  
市場競爭；反壟斷議題 ；企業 IT 支出或消費動能 趨緩。

### Page 6

美國 Amazon

十月 20, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 6

項目 定義 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用
事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下 
購買之再生能源 
Primary 再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源 
再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例 
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內 
廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物 
總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮 
環保支出 環保支出總金額 - 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處置、處理、衛生、清理支出 
員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減
或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數 
女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100 
女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100 
教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作 
每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數 
股東治理分數 衡量公司用以反收購工具的有效性 
公司治理分數 衡量公司對最佳治理原則的承諾和有效性 
產品責任分數 衡量公司生產優質產品和服務的能力，且產品是否將客戶的健康、安全、整合性和數據隱私進行綜合考量 
社區關係分數 衡量公司對成為優良公民、保護公眾健康和尊重商業道德的承諾 
勞動力分數 衡量公司在員工工作滿意度、健康、工作場所的安全、多樣性、平等以及員工發展機會方面的成效 
資源使用指標 衡量公司在原物料、能源或水的使用效率，以及是否通過改進供應鏈來尋求更俱生態效率的解決方案

### Page 1

十月 21, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  硬體  ‧美國

SpaceX 
(SPCX.OQ/SPCX US)

AI 算力租賃貢獻帶動營運由虧轉盈，並可望延續高成長性 
重要訊息 
SpaceX  3Q26 EPS 由虧轉盈並優於市場共識 ，主要來自 AI 業務轉盈；公司
給予 1Q27 營收較 3Q26 成長 140%之展望，盤後股價上漲約 7%。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 AI 營收跳升且轉盈，帶動 EPS 轉盈至 0.35 美元並優於共識。SpaceX 
3Q26 營收季增 86.5%至 145.8 億美元，動能來自 AI 業務營收季增 195%至
75.6 億美元，目前 AI 營收貢獻已超越衛星寬頻。在算力租賃契約陸續開始貢
獻下，3Q26 AI 業務之營業利益自 2Q26 虧損約 12.6 億美元轉盈至 4.3 億美
元，火箭業務亦隨公司增加 執行對外任務而使營業虧損收斂至 700 萬美元，
衛星寬頻業務亦隨用戶數增加而帶動營業利益季增 43%至 23.7 億美元，帶
動公司 EPS 轉盈至 0.35 美元，優於凱基與市場共識預估之 -0.12/0.24 美元。 
加速 AI 建置與 Starship 測試。4Q26 AI 算力租賃業務隨兩家不具名客戶分
別於 10 月與 12 月開始貢獻營收、單月分別貢獻約 11 億美元，預估帶動
4Q26 AI 業務營收季增 82%至 137 億美元。因應 AI 需求， SpaceX 規劃
4Q26 資本支出提升近 1 倍至 350-400 億美元，預計 4Q26 AI 建置量將自
3Q26 之 1.7GW 提升至 2.5-3GW，公司規劃算力約一半用於自用、一半租
賃，並提及新增算力預計在 1 年內回收投資成本。另外，第 15 次 Starship
試射預計於 11 月進行，此次將測試第一級推進器與第二級太空船回收能
力，並規劃 2027 年初測試第二節太空船復用能力，目前以 1H27 達到每週
發射 1 次 Starship、2H27 達到每週發射 2 次的頻率為目標。 
凱基上修 SpaceX  2026/2027 EPS 預估至-0.88/0.80 美元。基於 SpaceX AI
業務獲利改善狀況優於凱基原預期，且隨新客戶陸續貢獻，有助於 AI 營收
與獲利性逐步轉佳，故將 SpaceX 2026/2027 EPS 自原預估之- 1.64/ -0.57 美
元上修至- 0.88/0.80 美元。中長期而言，預期 SpaceX 主要獲利仍將來自衛
星寬頻與 AI 業務貢獻，凱基對長期財務預估大致不變。  
投資建議  
維持「增加持股」評等 ，目標價 227 美元(20 X FY2032  EPS 11.34 美元)。 
投資風險  
內部人持股陸續釋出， SpaceX 在外流通股數增加或為股價短期上行壓力。

### Page 1

十月 14, 2026 研究網站:  https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

產業報告  台灣

半導體產業

封測產業：資本支出與擴產規模創歷史新高  
重要訊息 
台灣封測廠 2026-27 年資本支出與擴產規模將創歷史新高。  
評論及分析  
數十年難得一見的成長機會。台灣前八大封測廠年初迄今已上修 2026 年資本
支出 45.9%至 4,839 億元，創歷史新高，較 2025 年幾乎翻倍(年增 99%，相
較過去 10 年複合年成長率 15.4% )。我們認為大幅擴產主要反映：( 1) AI 晶
片出貨強勁帶動既有業務成長；( 2)晶片尺寸擴大及設計複雜度提高，將 消耗
更多產能；(3)台積電(2330 TT ，NT$2,490，增加持股)將資源重新配置至先進
製程與先進封裝，並逐步退出晶圓測試(CP) 等毛利率較低或非核心業務。由
於上述成長動能均屬結構性因素，我們預期封測廠未來兩年資本支出仍將維
持高檔。  
先進封裝與晶圓測試需求龐大 。目前我們擁有的資訊仍不足以精確估算 AI 晶
片 CP 測試市場規模。然而，根據我們的初步估算及供應鏈調查，AI 晶片 CP
測試營收規模預估 達 500-600 億元，且將隨測試時間增加而持續成長。在假
設每投入 1 元資本支出可創造 0.5 元營收的情境下，我們預估 CP 測試所需資
本支出將超過 1,000 億元，占 2026 年封測廠整體資本支出 20-25%。我們預
估台灣封測廠資本密集度將由 2025 年的 28.9%升至 2026 年的 44.1%  (過去
10 年平均為 16.6% )，部分公司甚至超過 100%，遠高於半導體產業平均的
15.5%  (記憶體產業 2026 年營收預期成長超過 280%，拉低整體產業平均資
本密集度)。 
大規模擴產將加重財務負擔 。在封測廠中，以測試業務為主的公司因資本密
集度較高，故面臨的財務壓力可能更大，包括京元電子 (2449 TT，NT$296.5，
增加持股；資本密集度 104%)、欣銓(3264 T T，NT$278，未評等； 62%)及
台星科 (3265 TT ，NT$292.5，未評等；235%；其雖以封裝業務為主，但新
增產能主要用於 CP 測試)。而日月光投控(3711 TT ，NT$747，增加持股)整體
資本密集度相對同業較低，但若僅考量 AI 業務營收及相關擴產，資本密集度
亦超過 100%。我們認為封測廠資本支出仍 具進一步上修空間，因此多數業
者 2026- 27年將面臨龐大的資金需求 。 例如 ， 京元電子近期宣布與NVIDIA (美)
簽訂客戶預付款協議，以支應美國產能擴充。 NVIDIA 亦與 Amkor  (美)簽訂價
值 15 億美元的協議，透過預付款及多年期策略生產合作支持其擴產。我們預
期後續將有更多封測廠會 透過海外可轉債、聯貸、現金增資及可轉債來進行
籌資，預估 2027 年封測廠折舊費用將年增 30-100%。 
投資建議  
我們預期封測廠積極擴大資本支出與產能將有利於：(1) 設備供應商，包括
Advantest( 日)、鴻勁 (7769 TT，NT$5,780，增加持股) 及致茂(2360 TT ，
NT$2,250，未評等)；(2)測試介面供應商，包括中華精測 (6510 TT，NT$3,225 , 
增加持股)、穎崴(6515 TT，NT$5,615，增加持股)及旺矽(6223 TT，NT$5,885，
增加持股 )。封測族群中，我們看好 CPU 業務占比較高的日月光投控， 係因
NVIDIA V era Rubin 及 AMD (美) Venice 均將帶動其 CoWoS 全製程業務成長 ，
而台積電 2027-28 年持續擴產亦將為其載板端業務帶來成長空間 。 
投資風險  
若 AI 需求放緩，資本密集度及折舊費用較高 下，封測廠面臨的財務壓力將高
於其他半導體次產業。  
焦點內容

1. 數十年難得一見的成長機會。  
2. 先進封裝與晶圓測試需求龐大。  
3. 大規模擴產將加重財務負擔。

凱基投顧

### Page 3

原油
(株)日本総合研究所 原油市場展望 2026年９月
◆現状：100ドルを挟んで乱高下
８月のWTI原油先物価格は 、中旬に80ドル台
後半に上昇。米国とイランの交渉が難航したほか 、
イエメンの武装組織フーシがタンカー等を攻撃したこと
で、石油供給の減少が意識されたことが背景。下旬
には、80ドル台前半に下落 。ウォーシュ議長のタカ
派的な発言を受けて、FRBの利上げ観測が強まり、
米国需要が伸び悩むとの見方が広がったことが主因 。
８月末から９月中旬にかけて 、原油価格は100
ドル台に急騰。米国とイランの間で攻撃の応酬が続
いたことが背景。加えて、フーシがバブ・エル・マンデブ
海峡付近の港湾都市を掌握したことや 、サウジアラ
ビアの東西パイプラインが攻撃を受けて操業を停止し
たことで、ホルムズ海峡を迂回するルートからの石油
輸出が停滞するとの懸念も価格を押し上げ。下旬に
は、東西パイプラインの輸送能力が早期に回復する
との報道を材料に、原油価格は90ドル台に下落。
◆投機筋のネットポジションは小幅に拡大

投機筋の買い越し幅は拡大。中東情勢の緊迫化
を受けて、原油価格の上昇を見込む投資家が増加
している可能性。
◆見通し：当面100ドル前後で高止まり
両国の戦闘がこれ以上激化しないことを前提とす
るメインシナリオでは、原油価格が100ドルを中心と
する高値圏で推移する 見通し。ホルムズ海峡の封
鎖は続く一方、東西パイプラインなどの代替ルートや 、
中東産油国の生産が徐々に回復することが背景。

ＷＴＩ原油先物価格見通し

### Page 4

原油
(株)日本総合研究所 原油市場展望 2026年９月3
◆米国のガソリン・ディーゼル価格が高騰
米国では 、燃料油の価格が高騰 。足元のレギュ
ラーガソリン小売価格は全米平均で 4.5ドル/ガロン
と、消費者の心理的節目とされる４ドルを超える水
準。トラックや農業機械の燃料となるディーゼルの価
格は、統計開始以来の最高値に上昇。
◆製油業者のマージン確保や増税も影響
こうした石油製品価格が高騰する一因は 、原料と
なる原油の価格上昇。ただし、次の２点が、過去の
原油高局面よりも製品価格を高める方向に作用。
第１に、製油業者による強気のマージン設定 。世
界的な石油製品の需給ひっ迫が主因 。ホルムズ海
峡の封鎖を受けて 、中東からの製品輸出が減少し
たほか、主に中東産原油を精製していたアジアからの
輸出も減少。ウクライナによる石油関連インフラへの
攻撃を背景に、ロシアからの輸出も下振れ。
第２に、州政府による増税。連邦政府は、1993
年の増税以降、燃料税を据え置いている一方、多く
の州は、環境対策などの観点から増税を実施 。直

近の原油高局面（2022年）に比べて、州政府に
よる増税分が小売価格を押し上げる構図に。
◆燃料高は、米中間選挙で共和党の逆風に
燃料価格の高騰は、11月に実施される米中間選
挙において、現在の与党である共和党の逆風となる
可能性。ガソリン高が無党派層の不満を強めるほか、
ディーゼル高がトラック運転手や農家などの共和党の
伝統的な支持基盤の反発を招く可能性も。

Document 592: 叠纸低调搞起了机器人，七个月前已开始招兵买马

Source PDF: all report pdf/叠纸低调搞起了机器人，七个月前已开始招兵买马.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

靠虚拟⻆⾊和情感陪伴俘获⼤量玩家的游戏公司叠纸，正在尝试将这⻔⽣意延伸到现实世界。
10 ⽉ 16 ⽇，⼥娲仿⽣（上海）智能科技有限公司正式成⽴，注册资本 1000 万元，经营范围包括
智能机器⼈研发、服务消费机器⼈制造与销售、⼈⼯智能硬件销售等。
⼯商信息显⽰，叠纸创始⼈兼 CEO 姚润昊持股 60% ，叠纸科技法定代表⼈姚⻜直接持股 3% 。新
公司与叠纸科技并不存在直接股权关系。
这并⾮⼀次毫⽆征兆的跨界。
早在今年 3 ⽉，叠纸就开始集中招聘 AI 机器⼈结构负责⼈、 AI 陪伴玩具硬件⼯程师、⼯业设计和
原型专家，⼯作地点集中在上海杨浦区。
其中， “AI 陪伴产品⼯业设计师 ” 岗位，透露了更具体的产品⽅向。
岗位要求设计师按照 “ 外观先⾏ ” 的思路安排内部硬件结构，避免电⼦元件破坏产品的亲和⼒；同
时要求具备 3D 打印和基础缝纫能⼒，能够独⽴完成⽑绒外⽪与 3D 打印内部⽀架的组装。
8 ⽉ 27 ⽇，上海叠纸互娱⽹络科技有限公司⼜变更经营范围，新增⼈⼯智能软件开发、⼈⼯智能

硬件销售，以及玩具制造、玩具销售等业务。
从这些线索来看，叠纸最早推出的产品可能并⾮⼤众认知中的⼈形机器⼈，⽽是以⽑绒玩具为
外观、集成 AI 语⾳交互及简单机械结构的轻量化陪伴硬件。
这⼀动作并⾮孤例。拥有成熟⻆⾊ IP 的游戏公司，不少已经开始尝试将 AI 交互能⼒从数字世界延
伸⾄实体硬件。
其中，⽶哈游的布局与叠纸尤为接近。
2025 年 5 ⽉，⽶哈游开始招聘 AI 电⼦宠物产品设计专家，负责产品从概念到落地的全流程设计，
并明确提出优先考虑具有潮玩设计经验的⼈才。
此后，公司还招聘⾼级硬件结构⼯程师，负责桌⾯级⼩型智能硬件的结构设计，包括传动结
构、注塑⻣架及外观包覆件集成。
2026 年 8 ⽉，⽶哈游⼜在国内申请 MIYOMI 、 HoYo-PUNI 等商标，海外相关商标的商品描述则覆
盖 AI 机器⼈、智能玩具等类别。
游戏公司愿意进⼊这⼀陌⽣领域，与其⻓期积累的⻆⾊塑造和内容运营能⼒密切相关。
以叠纸为例，从《恋与制作⼈》到《恋与深空》，公司通过⼈物设定、剧情和实时互动，让玩家

与虚拟⻆⾊建⽴⻓期情感关系，并围绕这种关系形成持续付费。
但过去，玩家与⻆⾊的互动主要存在于⼿机屏幕中。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

1 条评论
AI 陪伴硬件则提供了另⼀种可能：让虚拟⻆⾊拥有实体载体，通过声⾳、动作和⽇常对话与玩家
交流，将游戏中的陪伴关系延伸到现实⽣活。
对于游戏公司⽽⾔，这项探索的意义可能不只是增加⼀种 IP 衍⽣品，⽽是尝试让⻆⾊摆脱对单款
游戏的依赖，形成更⻓的商业⽣命周期。
2025 年 12 ⽉，⽹易《逆⽔寒》⼿游与⾸形科技合作推出游戏⻆⾊ “ ⽅承意 ” 的 1:1 仿⽣机器⼈，通
过仿⽣⽪肤和⾯部驱动技术还原⻆⾊形象，并在杭州漫展亮相。
今年，⽹易《蛋仔派对》⼜将 IP 授权给 AI 硬件企业鲁⽶特，推出具备语⾳交流、表情反馈和⻓期
记忆能⼒的智能⽑绒玩具 “ 蛋⼩⻩ ” 。
相⽐之下，叠纸与⽶哈游选择组建⾃有硬件研发团队，更深⼊地参与产品设计与开发。

尽管需要承担更多研发和供应链⻛险，但也有机会掌握⻆⾊的实体交互体验，以及后续内容更
新和⽤⼾运营的主动权。
不过，游戏 IP 的影响⼒并不直接等同于硬件产品的市场需求。
玩家对熟悉⻆⾊的声⾳、性格和⾏为⽅式已经形成稳定预期。如何在价格可接受的前提下，还
原⻆⾊设定并提供⾃然、稳定的 AI 交互体验，将决定这些产品能否从粉丝周边进⼀步发展为具有
持续经营价值的消费硬件业务。

Document 605: 国内经济数据解读（2026年第三季度）：动能向新但结构问题突出

Source PDF: all report pdf/国内经济数据解读（2026年第三季度）：动能向新但结构问题突出.pdf
Report source: 浙商期货
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 15
Status: ok

### Page 1

国内经济数据解读（2026年第三季度）
动能向新但结构问题突出
宏观研究报告
2026-10-19
数据截至9月／三季度
浙商期货有限公司期货交易咨询业务资格：证监许可[2011]1287号
撰写人：朱枭鹏，咨询证号：Z0015158 ｜ 联系电话：0571-87219355
核心判断
观点结论：前三季度中国经济顶压前行、总体平稳，GDP同比增长4.6%、符合全年预期目标，三季度当季增速4.5%较二季度回升0.2个百
分点、环比增1.2%。结构上"动能向新、结构向优"——装备制造业、高技术制造业领跑生产，服务零售、网上零售较快增长，出口强劲
（前三季度+16.4%）成为需求端主要支撑；但内需结构性偏弱仍是核心矛盾：固定资产投资累计同比-7.2%、房地产开发投资-20.6%降
幅扩大，商品房销售仍低迷，消费虽边际改善但仍偏弱（9月社零+1.1%）。就业总体稳定（9月城镇调查失业率5.2%）。整体呈现"生产
强、出口旺、内需弱、地产拖累"的分化格局，需关注逆周期政策短期效果。
近期驱动：①政策基调：7月政治局会议明确"加大逆周期调节力度"，宏观政策要发力提效，财政货币协同（"六张网"建设、8000亿元新型政策

性金融工具、四季度加发5500亿元地方债）；②9月29日央行调整结构性货币政策工具，加大对基建投资、科技融资和中小微企业支持；③9月
制造业PMI 50.1重回扩张区间、服务业商务活动指数50.2；④外需强劲但内需不足问题仍较突出，官方定调"国际环境复杂多变，国内需求不足
问题仍较突出，经济稳中向好基础尚需巩固"。
浙商期货宏观研究团队

二、分项拆解：工业增加值
9月规模以上工业增加值同比+5.5%，比上月加快0.3个百分点，环比+0.61%，增速符合预期、保持较快增长。前三季度累计+5.3%，与1-8月持
平。分三大门类看，制造业+5.7%是主力（采矿业+2.0%、电力热力燃气水+5.4%）。结构上装备制造业增加值+10.2%、高技术制造业+14.3%，

### Page 3

分别快于全部规上工业4.9和9.0个百分点，新动能支撑明显；3D打印设备(+62.5%)、锂离子电池(+39.5%)、工业机器人(+29.1%)产量高增。9月制
造业PMI回升至50.1重新站上荣枯线，企业生产经营活动预期指数53.8。1-8月规上工业企业利润总额同比增长15.7%，盈利改善支撑生产。
图1 工业增加值累计同比季节图（2022年至今）

### Page 4

图2 工业增加值三大门类当月同比（近五年）
三、分项拆解：社会消费品零售总额

### Page 5

9月社零同比+1.1%，比上月加快0.7个百分点，环比+0.35%，好于市场预期（0.6%）。前三季度社零累计+1.1%、总额达37.0万亿元。结构上服
务消费韧性较强：前三季度社会消费商品和服务零售总额+2.6%，其中服务零售额+5.0%、网上零售较快增长（网上商品和服务零售额+4.4%）。
按类型看，商品零售+1.0%、餐饮收入+2.4%；限额以上粮油食品类(+6.8%)、通讯器材类(+18.1%)、文化办公用品类(+6.2%)增长较快。但汽车消
费持续偏弱，9月汽车类零售当月同比-21.2%，对社零拖累明显；除汽车以外的消费品零售额当月+3.9%，反映汽车是主要拖累项。整体消费呈弱修
复、结构性亮点（服务+升级类）特征，内需消费信心仍待政策提振。

### Page 6

图3 社会消费品零售总额累计同比季节图（2022年至今）
图4 社零及分项当月同比（近五年）

### Page 7

四、分项拆解：固定资产投资
前三季度固定资产投资（不含农户）累计同比-7.2%，与1-8月持平，略好于预期（-7.3%）。三大分项分化：房地产开发投资-20.6%（降幅较1-8
月扩大0.7pct，仍是最主要拖累）；制造业投资-2.1%（转负）；基础设施投资-4.2%（降幅扩大0.2pct）。结构亮点在于新动能：高技术产业投资
+4.9%（信息服务业+20.2%、航空航天器+12.5%、电子通信+7.6%），知识产权产品投资+9.3%；扣除房地产开发的固定资产投资下降4.2%。9
月固定资产投资环比+0.07%。整体看，传统地产、基建、制造业投资均偏弱，投资端主要靠高技术产业与新质生产力支撑，财政靠前发力（专项
债、政策性金融工具、四季度加发地方债）效果仍待实物工作量形成。

### Page 9

图6 三大投资分项累计同比（近五年）
五、分项拆解：房地产

### Page 10

房地产仍未止跌，是需求端最大拖累。前三季度房地产开发投资累计同比-20.6%，降幅持续扩大。三大施工链条全面承压：房屋施工面积累
计-12.8%、新开工面积-24.9%、竣工面积-23.7%，前端新开工与后端竣工均深度负增长。库存高企：商品房待售面积累计值7.53亿平方米（较
2021年低点已回升约50%）。销售端：前三季度新建商品房销售面积-13.2%、销售额-13.9%；但二手房交易网签面积+10.7%，显示"以旧换新"、
存量房消化背景下二手市场相对活跃。政策面上，7·30政治局会议再提"稳定房地产市场"、实施一揽子化债方案，四季度专项债及政策性金融工具或
加大"保交楼"与存量收购力度，但地产企稳仍需观察销售与资金面持续改善。
